# Third-party notices

MCP for NovelAI is licensed under the MIT License (see `LICENSE`). The application, its installers and its MCP server
bundle also contain the third-party software and data listed below, each under its own license; the MIT License of
this project does not relicense them.

This file is generated by `novelai/client/scripts/generate-notices.mjs` from the lockfile and the installed packages
(`npm --prefix novelai/client run notices`); do not edit it by hand. License texts are reproduced from each package's
own license files at the end of this file (§7); identical texts are printed once.

1. Electron (application runtime)
2. Client runtime dependencies (`app.asar/node_modules` and the renderer bundle `dist/assets`)
3. Image processing binaries (sharp and libvips, platform-specific)
4. MCP server bundle (`resources/mcp/novelai-mcp.mjs`)
5. Build tool runtime code
6. Data
7. License texts

## 1. Electron

| Component | Version | License | Project | License text |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| electron | 42.11.0 | MIT | https://github.com/electron/electron | [L1](#l1) (LICENSE) |

Electron contains Chromium, Node.js, V8 and further open-source components under their own licenses, among them
FFmpeg under the GNU LGPL. Their notices are in `LICENSES.chromium.html`, which ships with the app: on Windows and
Linux in the installation folder (next to `LICENSE.electron.txt`), on macOS in
`MCP for NovelAI.app/Contents/Resources`. The app uses Electron's own binaries unmodified; their source code is that
of Electron v42.11.0 (https://github.com/electron/electron/tree/v42.11.0 and the Chromium and other
sources its `DEPS` file names). It is not attached to this project's releases.

## 2. Client runtime dependencies

Every installer contains the client's third-party code in two places, listed in §2.1 and §2.2 (a package in both is
listed in both).

### 2.2 Renderer bundle (`dist/assets`)

The user interface is bundled by Vite into `dist/assets` inside `app.asar`. Its third-party code comes from these
packages, found by bundling the renderer entry `src/main.tsx` and counting a package only when some of its code
reaches the output. A package only the bundle needs is a development dependency of the client, so electron-builder
does not copy it into `app.asar/node_modules`; one the main process needs as well is also in §2.1. 12 packages:

| Package | Version | License | Project | License text |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| @tanstack/query-core | 5.102.8 | MIT | https://github.com/TanStack/query | [L57](#l57) (LICENSE) |
| @tanstack/react-query | 5.102.8 | MIT | https://github.com/TanStack/query | [L57](#l57) (LICENSE) |
| ajv | 8.20.0 | MIT | https://github.com/ajv-validator/ajv | [L6](#l6) (LICENSE) |
| clsx | 2.1.1 | MIT | https://github.com/lukeed/clsx | [L58](#l58) (license) |
| fast-deep-equal | 3.1.3 | MIT | https://github.com/epoberezkin/fast-deep-equal | [L18](#l18) (LICENSE) |
| fast-uri | 3.1.6 | BSD-3-Clause | https://github.com/fastify/fast-uri | [L19](#l19) (LICENSE) |
| json-schema-traverse | 1.0.0 | MIT | https://github.com/epoberezkin/json-schema-traverse | [L18](#l18) (LICENSE) |
| react | 19.2.7 | MIT | https://github.com/facebook/react | [L59](#l59) (LICENSE) |
| react-dom | 19.2.7 | MIT | https://github.com/facebook/react | [L59](#l59) (LICENSE) |
| react-icons | 5.7.0 | MIT | https://github.com/react-icons/react-icons | [L60](#l60) (LICENSE) |
| scheduler | 0.27.0 | MIT | https://github.com/facebook/react | [L59](#l59) (LICENSE) |
| zustand | 5.0.14 | MIT | https://github.com/pmndrs/zustand | [L61](#l61) (LICENSE) |

Notes:
- Of react-icons the renderer uses only the Material Design icons (`react-icons/md`), which are Google's Material
  Design icons under the Apache License 2.0; react-icons' own LICENSE lists the licenses of its icon sets.

## 3. Image processing binaries (sharp and libvips)

sharp 0.35.4 (Apache-2.0, https://github.com/lovell/sharp) loads prebuilt native binaries from platform
packages; npm installs the ones matching the operating system and CPU it installs for. The installers this project
builds contain: Windows x64 (NSIS installer) `@img/sharp-win32-x64`, which carries libvips itself; Linux x64
(tar.gz) `@img/sharp-linux-x64` with `@img/sharp-libvips-linux-x64`; macOS, one installer per architecture (dmg
and zip), the darwin pair of its architecture: `@img/sharp-darwin-arm64` with `@img/sharp-libvips-darwin-arm64` in
the arm64 installer (Apple silicon) and `@img/sharp-darwin-x64` with `@img/sharp-libvips-darwin-x64` in the x64
installer (Intel). The lockfile names these variants (and what only they require):

| Package | Version | License | Platform |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| @emnapi/runtime | 1.11.3 | MIT | only as a dependency of a WebAssembly variant |
| @img/sharp-darwin-arm64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | darwin arm64 |
| @img/sharp-darwin-x64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | darwin x64 |
| @img/sharp-freebsd-wasm32 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | freebsd |
| @img/sharp-libvips-darwin-arm64 | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | darwin arm64 |
| @img/sharp-libvips-darwin-x64 | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | darwin x64 |
| @img/sharp-libvips-linux-arm | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | linux arm |
| @img/sharp-libvips-linux-arm64 | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | linux arm64 |
| @img/sharp-libvips-linux-ppc64 | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | linux ppc64 |
| @img/sharp-libvips-linux-riscv64 | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | linux riscv64 |
| @img/sharp-libvips-linux-s390x | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | linux s390x |
| @img/sharp-libvips-linux-x64 | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | linux x64 |
| @img/sharp-libvips-linuxmusl-arm64 | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | linux arm64 |
| @img/sharp-libvips-linuxmusl-x64 | 1.3.3 | LGPL-3.0-or-later | linux x64 |
| @img/sharp-linux-arm | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | linux arm |
| @img/sharp-linux-arm64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | linux arm64 |
| @img/sharp-linux-ppc64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | linux ppc64 |
| @img/sharp-linux-riscv64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | linux riscv64 |
| @img/sharp-linux-s390x | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | linux s390x |
| @img/sharp-linux-x64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | linux x64 |
| @img/sharp-linuxmusl-arm64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | linux arm64 |
| @img/sharp-linuxmusl-x64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | linux x64 |
| @img/sharp-wasm32 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 AND LGPL-3.0-or-later AND MIT | only as a dependency of a WebAssembly variant |
| @img/sharp-webcontainers-wasm32 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 | wasm32 |
| @img/sharp-win32-arm64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 AND LGPL-3.0-or-later | win32 arm64 |
| @img/sharp-win32-ia32 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 AND LGPL-3.0-or-later | win32 ia32 |
| @img/sharp-win32-x64 | 0.35.4 | Apache-2.0 AND LGPL-3.0-or-later | win32 x64 |
| tslib | 2.8.1 | 0BSD | only as a dependency of a WebAssembly variant |

The `@img/sharp-<platform>` packages contain sharp's compiled addon under the same Apache-2.0 license as sharp
([L51](#l51); their own LICENSE files are identical). The `@img/sharp-libvips-<platform>` packages (and the libvips part of the Windows and
WebAssembly packages) contain libvips 8.18.6 and the libraries listed in §3.1, built by the sharp-libvips
project 1.3.3. libvips and several of those libraries are licensed under the GNU Lesser General
Public License version 3 or later (LGPL-3.0-or-later); its text and the GNU GPL it incorporates are in
[L62](#l62) and [L63](#l63) (from https://www.gnu.org/licenses/). In the native packages libvips is a separate shared
library (`lib/libvips-*`) that sharp loads at run time.

Source code: libvips 8.18.6 at https://github.com/libvips/libvips/tree/v8.18.6; the build scripts and
the exact library versions of these binaries at https://github.com/lovell/sharp-libvips/tree/v1.3.3
(releases: https://github.com/lovell/sharp-libvips/releases/tag/v1.3.3); sharp at
https://github.com/lovell/sharp/tree/v0.35.4. The source archive of every library is linked in §3.1.

Every release of this project (https://github.com/lwyq1209/mcp-for-novelai/releases) provides this source code with
its installers, as `third-party-sources-<version>.tar.gz`: the source archive of every library in §3.1 (each checked
against the license files reproduced in §3.2), the build scripts of sharp-libvips 1.3.3 and of
libvips/build-win64-mxe v8.18.6, and the MPL-2.0 Rust crates of §3.3, with a list of
the archives, their upstream URLs and sha256.

| zlib-ng | 2.3.3 | zlib License (https://github.com/zlib-ng/zlib-ng/blob/develop/LICENSE.md) | https://github.com/zlib-ng/zlib-ng/archive/2.3.3.tar.gz | [V38](#v38) (LICENSE.md) |

Notes:
- cairo's own files offer it under LGPL-2.1 or MPL-1.1 (`COPYING`); sharp-libvips states MPL-2.0. Both cairo files
  and the MPL-2.0 text (https://www.mozilla.org/media/MPL/2.0/index.txt) are reproduced.
- libnsgif is compiled into libvips from libvips' own source tree (`libvips/foreign/libnsgif`).
- FreeType is built with its default modules, which include the BDF and PCF font drivers. As FreeType's
  `LICENSE.TXT` says, these drivers (`src/bdf`, `src/pcf`) and its hash functions (`src/base/fthash.c`,
  `include/freetype/internal/fthash.h`) are under their own X11-style license; their license texts (the drivers'
  READMEs and the license comment of `fthash.c`) are reproduced in §3.2.
- librsvg is written in Rust: the Rust crates it is built from and the Rust standard library are compiled into the
  libvips binaries too. They are listed in §3.3.
- The Windows libvips (`@img/sharp-win32-x64`, built by build-win64-mxe with an LLVM-based MinGW toolchain) also contains
  code of LLVM's C++ runtime libraries libc++ and libc++abi 22.1.7 (https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/tree/llvmorg-22.1.7),
  under the Apache License 2.0 with LLVM Exceptions (Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception). It is embedded in the binary
  by compiling, which that exception exempts from the license's notice and attribution conditions.

Acknowledgements these licenses require:
- This software is based in part on the work of the Independent JPEG Group. (mozjpeg, IJG License)
- Portions of this software are copyright © 2026 The FreeType Project (https://freetype.org). All rights reserved. (FreeType 2.14.3, FreeType License)
- This product includes Gain Map technology under license by Adobe. (libultrahdr 2.0.2, NOTICE)

Licensing of the bundled libraries, as stated by sharp-libvips (its `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` of v1.3.3):

```text
/*
 * This code implements the MD5 message-digest algorithm.
 * The algorithm is due to Ron Rivest.  This code was
 * written by Colin Plumb in 1993, no copyright is claimed.
 * This code is in the public domain; do with it what you wish.
 *
 * Equivalent code is available from RSA Data Security, Inc.
 * This code has been tested against that, and is equivalent,
 * except that you don't need to include two pages of legalese
 * with every copy.
 *
 * To compute the message digest of a chunk of bytes, declare an
 * MD5Context structure, pass it to MD5Init, call MD5Update as
 * needed on buffers full of bytes, and then call MD5Final, which
 * will fill a supplied 16-byte array with the digest.
 */

fontconfig/src/ftglue.[ch]

/* ftglue.c: Glue code for compiling the OpenType code from
 *           FreeType 1 using only the public API of FreeType 2
 *
 * By David Turner, The FreeType Project (www.freetype.org)
 *
 * This code is explicitely put in the public domain
 *
 * ==========================================================================
 *
 * the OpenType parser codes was originally written as an extension to
 * FreeType 1.x. As such, its source code was embedded within the library,
 * and used many internal FreeType functions to deal with memory and
 * stream i/o.
 *
 * When it was 'salvaged' for Pango and Qt, the code was "ported" to FreeType 2,
 * which basically means that some macro tricks were performed in order to
 * directly access FT2 _internal_ functions.
 *
 * these functions were never part of FT2 public API, and _did_ change between
 * various releases. This created chaos for many users: when they upgraded the
 * FreeType library on their system, they couldn't run Gnome anymore since
 * Pango refused to link.
 *
 * Very fortunately, it's possible to completely avoid this problem because
 * the FT_StreamRec and FT_MemoryRec structure types, which describe how
 * memory and stream implementations interface with the rest of the font
 * library, have always been part of the public API, and never changed.
 *
 * What we do thus is re-implement, within the OpenType parser, the few
 * functions that depend on them. This only adds one or two kilobytes of
 * code, and ensures that the parser can work with _any_ version
 * of FreeType installed on your system. How sweet... !
 *
 * Note that we assume that Pango doesn't use any other internal functions
 * from FreeType. It used to in old versions, but this should no longer
 * be the case. (crossing my fingers).
 *
 *  - David Turner
 *  - The FreeType Project  (www.freetype.org)
 *
 * PS: This "glue" code is explicitely put in the public domain
 */
```

#### V15

Used by: freetype 2.14.3

```text
                  FreeType font driver for BDF fonts

                       Francesco Zappa Nardelli
                  <francesco.zappa.nardelli@ens.fr>


Introduction
************

BDF (Bitmap Distribution Format) is a bitmap font format defined by Adobe,
which is intended to be easily understood by both humans and computers.
This code implements a BDF driver for the FreeType library, following the
Adobe Specification V 2.2.  The specification of the BDF font format is
available from Adobe's web site:

  https://adobe-type-tools.github.io/font-tech-notes/pdfs/5005.BDF_Spec.pdf

Many good bitmap fonts in bdf format come with XFree86 (www.XFree86.org).
They do not define vertical metrics, because the X Consortium BDF
specification has removed them.


Encodings
*********

[This section is out of date, retained for historical reasons.  BDF
 properties can be retrieved with `FT_Get_BDF_Property`, character set ID
 values with `FT_Get_BDF_Charset_ID`.]

The variety of encodings that accompanies bdf fonts appears to encompass the
small set defined in freetype.h.  On the other hand, two properties that
specify encoding and registry are usually defined in bdf fonts.

I decided to make these two properties directly accessible, leaving to the
client application the work of interpreting them.  For instance:


  #include FT_INTERNAL_BDF_TYPES_H

  FT_Face          face;
  BDF_Public_Face  bdfface;


  FT_New_Face( library, ..., &face );

  bdfface = (BDF_Public_Face)face;

  if ( ( bdfface->charset_registry == "ISO10646" ) &&
       ( bdfface->charset_encoding == "1" )        )
    [..]


Thus the driver always exports `ft_encoding_none' as face->charmap.encoding.
FT_Get_Char_Index's behavior is unmodified, that is, it converts the ULong
value given as argument into the corresponding glyph number.

If the two properties are not available, Adobe Standard Encoding should be
assumed.


Anti-Aliased Bitmaps
********************

The driver supports an extension to the BDF format as used in Mark Leisher's
xmbdfed bitmap font editor.  Microsoft's SBIT tool expects bitmap fonts in
that format for adding anti-aliased them to TrueType fonts.  It introduces a
fourth field to the `SIZE' keyword which gives the bpp value (bits per
pixel) of the glyph data in the font.  Possible values are 1 (the default),
2 (four gray levels), 4 (16 gray levels), and 8 (256 gray levels).  The
driver returns either a bitmap with 1 bit per pixel or a pixmap with 8bits
per pixel (using 4, 16, and 256 gray levels, respectively).


Known problems
**************

- A font is entirely loaded into memory.  Obviously, this is not the Right
  Thing(TM).  If you have big fonts I suggest you convert them into PCF
  format (using the bdftopcf utility): the PCF font drive of FreeType can
  perform incremental glyph loading.

When I have some time, I will implement on-demand glyph parsing.

- Except for encodings properties, client applications have no visibility of
  the PCF_Face object.  This means that applications cannot directly access
  font tables and must trust FreeType.

- Currently, glyph names are ignored.

  I plan to give full visibility of the BDF_Face object in an upcoming
  revision of the driver, thus implementing also glyph names.

- As I have never seen a BDF font that defines vertical metrics, vertical
  metrics are (parsed and) discarded.  If you own a BDF font that defines
  vertical metrics, please let me know (I will implement them in 5-10
  minutes).


License
*******

Copyright (C) 2001-2002 by Francesco Zappa Nardelli

Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining
a copy of this software and associated documentation files (the
"Software"), to deal in the Software without restriction, including
without limitation the rights to use, copy, modify, merge, publish,
distribute, sublicense, and/or sell copies of the Software, and to
permit persons to whom the Software is furnished to do so, subject to
the following conditions:

The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be
included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.

THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT WARRANTY OF ANY KIND,
EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF
MERCHANTABILITY, FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT.
IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY
CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT,
TORT OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE
SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.

*** Portions of the driver (that is, bdflib.c and bdf.h):

Copyright 2000 Computing Research Labs, New Mexico State University
Copyright 2001-2002, 2011 Francesco Zappa Nardelli

Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining a
copy of this software and associated documentation files (the "Software"),
to deal in the Software without restriction, including without limitation
the rights to use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense,
and/or sell copies of the Software, and to permit persons to whom the
Software is furnished to do so, subject to the following conditions:

The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in
all copies or substantial portions of the Software.

THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT WARRANTY OF ANY KIND, EXPRESS OR
IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY,
FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT.  IN NO EVENT SHALL
THE COMPUTING RESEARCH LAB OR NEW MEXICO STATE UNIVERSITY BE LIABLE FOR ANY
CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT, TORT
OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR
THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.


Credits
*******

This driver is based on excellent Mark Leisher's bdf library.  If you
find something good in this driver you should probably thank him, not
me.
```

#### V16

Used by: freetype 2.14.3

```text
                  FreeType font driver for PCF fonts

                       Francesco Zappa Nardelli
                  <francesco.zappa.nardelli@ens.fr>


Introduction
************

PCF (Portable Compiled Format) is a binary bitmap font format, largely used
in X world. This code implements a PCF driver for the FreeType library.
Glyph images are loaded into memory only on demand, thus leading to a small
memory footprint.

Information on the PCF font format can only be worked out from
`pcfread.c', and `pcfwrite.c', to be found, for instance, in the XFree86
(www.xfree86.org) source tree (xc/lib/font/bitmap/).

Many good bitmap fonts in bdf format come with XFree86: they can be
compiled into the pcf format using the `bdftopcf' utility.


Supported hardware
******************

The driver has been tested on linux/x86 and sunos5.5/sparc.  In both
cases the compiler was gcc.  When back in Paris, I will test it also
on linux/alpha.


Encodings
*********

Use `FT_Get_BDF_Charset_ID' to access the encoding and registry.

The driver always exports `ft_encoding_none' as face->charmap.encoding.
FT_Get_Char_Index() behavior is unmodified, that is, it converts the ULong
value given as argument into the corresponding glyph number.


Known problems
**************

- dealing explicitly with encodings breaks the uniformity of FreeType 2
  API.

- except for encodings properties, client applications have no
  visibility of the PCF_Face object.  This means that applications
  cannot directly access font tables and are obliged to trust
  FreeType.

- currently, glyph names and ink_metrics are ignored.

I plan to give full visibility of the PCF_Face object in the next
release of the driver, thus implementing also glyph names and
ink_metrics.

- height is defined as (ascent - descent).  Is this correct?

- if unable to read size information from the font, PCF_Init_Face
  sets available_size->width and available_size->height to 12.

- too many english grammar errors in the readme file :-(


License
*******

Copyright (C) 2000 by Francesco Zappa Nardelli

Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining
a copy of this software and associated documentation files (the
"Software"), to deal in the Software without restriction, including
without limitation the rights to use, copy, modify, merge, publish,
distribute, sublicense, and/or sell copies of the Software, and to
permit persons to whom the Software is furnished to do so, subject to
the following conditions:

The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be
included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.

THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT WARRANTY OF ANY KIND,
EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF
MERCHANTABILITY, FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT.
IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY
CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT,
TORT OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE
SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.


Credits
*******

Keith Packard wrote the pcf driver found in XFree86.  His work is at
the same time the specification and the sample implementation of the
PCF format.  Undoubtedly, this driver is inspired from his work.
```

```text
Please read at least usage.txt.  Some information can also be found in the JPEG

If you want to understand how the JPEG code works, we suggest reading one or
more of the REFERENCES, then looking at the documentation files (in roughly
the order listed) before diving into the code.


OVERVIEW
========

This package contains C software to implement JPEG image encoding, decoding,
and transcoding.  JPEG (pronounced "jay-peg") is a standardized compression
method for full-color and grayscale images.  JPEG's strong suit is compressing
photographic images or other types of images that have smooth color and
brightness transitions between neighboring pixels.  Images with sharp lines or
other abrupt features may not compress well with JPEG, and a higher JPEG
quality may have to be used to avoid visible compression artifacts with such
images.

JPEG is normally lossy, meaning that the output pixels are not necessarily
identical to the input pixels.  However, on photographic content and other
"smooth" images, very good compression ratios can be obtained with no visible
compression artifacts, and extremely high compression ratios are possible if
you are willing to sacrifice image quality (by reducing the "quality" setting
in the compressor.)

This software implements JPEG baseline, extended-sequential, progressive, and
lossless compression processes.  Provision is made for supporting all variants
of these processes, although some uncommon parameter settings aren't
implemented yet.  We have made no provision for supporting the hierarchical
processes defined in the standard.

We provide a set of library routines for reading and writing JPEG image files,
plus two sample applications "cjpeg" and "djpeg", which use the library to
perform conversion between JPEG and some other popular image file formats.
The library is intended to be reused in other applications.

In order to support file conversion and viewing software, we have included
considerable functionality beyond the bare JPEG coding/decoding capability;
for example, the color quantization modules are not strictly part of JPEG
decoding, but they are essential for output to colormapped file formats.  These
extra functions can be compiled out of the library if not required for a
particular application.

We have also included "jpegtran", a utility for lossless transcoding between
different JPEG processes, and "rdjpgcom" and "wrjpgcom", two simple
applications for inserting and extracting textual comments in JFIF files.

The emphasis in designing this software has been on achieving portability and
flexibility, while also making it fast enough to be useful.  In particular,
the software is not intended to be read as a tutorial on JPEG.  (See the
REFERENCES section for introductory material.)  Rather, it is intended to
be reliable, portable, industrial-strength code.  We do not claim to have
achieved that goal in every aspect of the software, but we strive for it.

We welcome the use of this software as a component of commercial products.
No royalty is required, but we do ask for an acknowledgement in product
documentation, as described under LEGAL ISSUES.


LEGAL ISSUES
============

REFERENCES
==========

We recommend reading one or more of these references before trying to
understand the innards of the JPEG software.

The best short technical introduction to the JPEG compression algorithm is
        Wallace, Gregory K.  "The JPEG Still Picture Compression Standard",
        Communications of the ACM, April 1991 (vol. 34 no. 4), pp. 30-44.
(Adjacent articles in that issue discuss MPEG motion picture compression,
applications of JPEG, and related topics.)  If you don't have the CACM issue
handy, a PDF file containing a revised version of Wallace's article is
available at http://www.ijg.org/files/Wallace.JPEG.pdf.  The file (actually
a preprint for an article that appeared in IEEE Trans. Consumer Electronics)
omits the sample images that appeared in CACM, but it includes corrections
and some added material.  Note: the Wallace article is copyright ACM and IEEE,
and it may not be used for commercial purposes.

A somewhat less technical, more leisurely introduction to JPEG can be found in
"The Data Compression Book" by Mark Nelson and Jean-loup Gailly, published by
M&T Books (New York), 2nd ed. 1996, ISBN 1-55851-434-1.  This book provides
good explanations and example C code for a multitude of compression methods
including JPEG.  It is an excellent source if you are comfortable reading C
code but don't know much about data compression in general.  The book's JPEG
sample code is far from industrial-strength, but when you are ready to look
at a full implementation, you've got one here...

The best currently available description of JPEG is the textbook "JPEG Still
Image Data Compression Standard" by William B. Pennebaker and Joan L.
Mitchell, published by Van Nostrand Reinhold, 1993, ISBN 0-442-01272-1.
Price US$59.95, 638 pp.  The book includes the complete text of the ISO JPEG
standards (DIS 10918-1 and draft DIS 10918-2).

The original JPEG standard is divided into two parts, Part 1 being the actual
specification, while Part 2 covers compliance testing methods.  Part 1 is
titled "Digital Compression and Coding of Continuous-tone Still Images,
Part 1: Requirements and guidelines" and has document numbers ISO/IEC IS
10918-1, ITU-T T.81.  Part 2 is titled "Digital Compression and Coding of
Continuous-tone Still Images, Part 2: Compliance testing" and has document
numbers ISO/IEC IS 10918-2, ITU-T T.83.

The JPEG standard does not specify all details of an interchangeable file
format.  For the omitted details, we follow the "JFIF" conventions, revision
1.02.  JFIF version 1 has been adopted as ISO/IEC 10918-5 (05/2013) and
Recommendation ITU-T T.871 (05/2011): Information technology - Digital
compression and coding of continuous-tone still images: JPEG File Interchange
Format (JFIF).  It is available as a free download in PDF file format from
https://www.iso.org/standard/54989.html and http://www.itu.int/rec/T-REC-T.871.
A PDF file of the older JFIF 1.02 specification is available at
http://www.w3.org/Graphics/JPEG/jfif3.pdf.

The TIFF 6.0 file format specification can be obtained from
http://mirrors.ctan.org/graphics/tiff/TIFF6.ps.gz.  The JPEG incorporation
scheme found in the TIFF 6.0 spec of 3-June-92 has a number of serious
problems.  IJG does not recommend use of the TIFF 6.0 design (TIFF Compression
tag 6).  Instead, we recommend the JPEG design proposed by TIFF Technical Note
#2 (Compression tag 7).  Copies of this Note can be obtained from
http://www.ijg.org/files/.  It is expected that the next revision
of the TIFF spec will replace the 6.0 JPEG design with the Note's design.
Although IJG's own code does not support TIFF/JPEG, the free libtiff library
uses our library to implement TIFF/JPEG per the Note.


ARCHIVE LOCATIONS
=================

The "official" archive site for this software is www.ijg.org.
The most recent released version can always be found there in
directory "files".

The JPEG FAQ (Frequently Asked Questions) article is a source of some
general information about JPEG.  It is available at
http://www.faqs.org/faqs/jpeg-faq.


FILE FORMAT COMPATIBILITY
=========================

This software implements ITU T.81 | ISO/IEC 10918 with some extensions from
ITU T.871 | ISO/IEC 10918-5 (JPEG File Interchange Format-- see REFERENCES).
Informally, the term "JPEG image" or "JPEG file" most often refers to JFIF or
a subset thereof, but there are other formats containing the name "JPEG" that
are incompatible with the original JPEG standard or with JFIF (for instance,
JPEG 2000 and JPEG XR).  This software therefore does not support these
formats.  Indeed, one of the original reasons for developing this free software
was to help force convergence on a common, interoperable format standard for
JPEG files.

JFIF is a minimal or "low end" representation.  TIFF/JPEG (TIFF revision 6.0 as
modified by TIFF Technical Note #2) can be used for "high end" applications
that need to record a lot of additional data about an image.


TO DO
=====

Please send bug reports, offers of help, etc. to jpeg-info@jpegclub.org.
```

### 3.3 Rust code in librsvg

librsvg 2.62.91 is written in Rust. The libvips binaries link it statically, so the Rust crates it is built from
and the Rust standard library are compiled into them, besides librsvg's own crates (librsvg and librsvg-c, under
librsvg's `COPYING.LIB` in §3.1). The crates are those sharp-libvips' build (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/lovell/sharp-libvips/v1.3.3/build/posix.sh) links: the
librsvg tarball (https://download.gnome.org/sources/librsvg/2.62/librsvg-2.62.91.tar.xz) with the Cargo.toml edits of that script and its regenerated lockfile, the
`librsvg-c` package with its default features, normal dependencies only (no build scripts, build tools or proc
macros, which run only while building). "Targets" names the binaries a crate is in: linux-x64, darwin-arm64, darwin-x64
and win32-x64 (Rust targets x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu, aarch64-apple-darwin, x86_64-apple-darwin, x86_64-pc-windows-gnu), "all" for every one. The Windows libvips is built by
build-win64-mxe (https://github.com/libvips/build-win64-mxe/releases/tag/v8.18.6), whose crate set may differ slightly from this list, which is
computed from sharp-libvips' build.

"License" is the license each crate declares in its Cargo.toml; the license files of each crate's package (crates.io)
are reproduced in §3.4. 131 crates (MIT OR Apache-2.0 56, MIT 28, Unicode-3.0 15, MIT/Apache-2.0 6, Apache-2.0 OR MIT 4, MPL-2.0 4, Zlib OR Apache-2.0 OR MIT 4, Apache-2.0 3, MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR Zlib 3, Unlicense OR MIT 3, BSD-3-Clause OR Apache-2.0 2, (Apache-2.0 OR MIT) AND BSD-3-Clause 1, 0BSD OR MIT OR Apache-2.0 1, MIT OR Zlib OR Apache-2.0 1):

| Crate | Version | License (declared) | Targets | Source | License files |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| zune-jpeg | 0.5.15 | MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR Zlib | all | https://crates.io/crates/zune-jpeg/0.5.15 | [R7](#r7) (LICENSE-APACHE), [R82](#r82) (LICENSE-MIT), [R83](#r83) (LICENSE-ZLIB) |
| Rust standard library | 1.100.0 (commit 787af2b8c80638c51a4fc8e44f84e6891f243ec7) | MIT OR Apache-2.0 (as stated in its COPYRIGHT) | all | https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/tree/787af2b8c80638c51a4fc8e44f84e6891f243ec7/library | [R84](#r84) (LICENSE-MIT), [R24](#r24) (LICENSE-APACHE), [R85](#r85) (COPYRIGHT) |

Notes:
- These crates ship no license file: block 0.1.6 (MIT; authors: Steven Sheldon); malloc_buf 0.0.6 (MIT; authors: Steven Sheldon); objc_id 0.1.1 (MIT; authors: Steven Sheldon); objc-foundation 0.1.1 (MIT; authors: Steven Sheldon); selectors 0.40.0 (MPL-2.0; authors: The Servo Project Developers); winapi-x86_64-pc-windows-gnu 0.4.0 (MIT/Apache-2.0; authors: Peter Atashian <retep998@gmail.com>).
  Their copyright holders are the authors listed in their Cargo.toml. In place of a license file the table links the
  standard text of the declared license: for MIT the SPDX MIT License text without a copyright line (the copyright
  is that of the crate's listed authors), for MPL-2.0 the text in §3.2, for Apache-2.0 the text in §7.
- cssparser 0.37.0, cssparser-color 0.5.0, dtoa-short 0.3.5, selectors 0.40.0 are under the Mozilla Public License 2.0; their source code is
  available at the crates.io source link given in the table.
- The Rust standard library is that of the Rust toolchain the binaries were compiled with (commit 787af2b8c80638c51a4fc8e44f84e6891f243ec7,
  `src/version` 1.100.0); its `COPYRIGHT`, `LICENSE-MIT` and `LICENSE-APACHE` at that commit are reproduced.
  The standard library is itself built from further crates; they are listed in `library/Cargo.lock` at that commit
  (https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/blob/787af2b8c80638c51a4fc8e44f84e6891f243ec7/library/Cargo.lock) and, as its `COPYRIGHT` says, in the
  `COPYRIGHT-library.html` of that toolchain; their license texts are not reproduced here.

### 3.4 License files of the Rust crates

Each file as it is in the crate's package (sha256 in `scripts/licenses/sharp-libvips/rsvg-crates.json`); identical
files are printed once.

## 4. MCP server bundle

`resources/mcp/novelai-mcp.mjs` is a single file built with esbuild from `novelai/src/mcp.mjs`. Besides this
project's own code it contains the following packages (from the esbuild metafile); the file carries no license
texts of its own, so this section is its notice. 8 packages:

| Package | Version | License | Project | License text |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| @modelcontextprotocol/sdk | 1.30.0 | MIT | https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/typescript-sdk | [L64](#l64) (LICENSE) |
| ajv | 8.20.0 | MIT | https://github.com/ajv-validator/ajv | [L6](#l6) (LICENSE) |
| ajv-formats | 3.0.1 | MIT | https://github.com/ajv-validator/ajv-formats | [L65](#l65) (LICENSE) |
| fast-deep-equal | 3.1.3 | MIT | https://github.com/epoberezkin/fast-deep-equal | [L18](#l18) (LICENSE) |
| fast-uri | 3.1.7 | BSD-3-Clause | https://github.com/fastify/fast-uri | [L19](#l19) (LICENSE) |
| json-schema-traverse | 1.0.0 | MIT | https://github.com/epoberezkin/json-schema-traverse | [L18](#l18) (LICENSE) |
| zod | 4.6.1 | MIT | https://github.com/colinhacks/zod | [L66](#l66) (LICENSE) |
| zod-to-json-schema | 3.25.2 | ISC | https://github.com/StefanTerdell/zod-to-json-schema | [L67](#l67) (LICENSE) |

The tag-module bundle `dist-electron/vendor/tag-details.cjs` (built from `novelai/src/tag-details.mjs`) contains
only this project's own code.

## 5. Build tool runtime code

The bundlers add small runtime helpers of their own to the code they emit:

| Tool | Version | License | Project | Use | License text |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| vite | 8.0.16 | MIT | https://github.com/vitejs/vite | Module-preload and dynamic-import helpers in the renderer bundle (dist/assets). | [L68](#l68) (LICENSE.md) |
| rolldown | 1.0.3 | MIT | https://github.com/rolldown/rolldown | Module runtime helpers in the renderer bundle (Vite 8 bundles with Rolldown). | [L69](#l69) (LICENSE) |
| esbuild | 0.28.2 | MIT | https://github.com/evanw/esbuild | CommonJS/ES module interop helpers in the MCP server bundle and the tag-module bundle. | [L70](#l70) (LICENSE.md) |

## 6. Data

Data compiled into the app, and where it comes from as far as known. The upstream project is Langbai NovelAI Studio
(https://github.com/2786886095/novelai-image-desktop, MIT, Copyright (c) 2026 Langbai).

- `electron/data/tag-dictionary.ts` — offline Chinese → Danbooru tag dictionary for prompt autocomplete (134 entries: English tag name, approximate post count, category, Chinese label and synonyms).
  Source: Provided with the MIT-licensed source code of the upstream Langbai NovelAI Studio (https://github.com/2786886095/novelai-image-desktop); the English tag names and categories are Danbooru tags.
- `src/related-tags.ts` — related-tag suggestions (78 English tags with Chinese labels, grouped by anchor tag).
  Source: Provided with the MIT-licensed source code of the upstream Langbai NovelAI Studio (https://github.com/2786886095/novelai-image-desktop), where it is described as curated Danbooru co-occurrence.
- `src/prompt-data.ts` — Chinese glosses for common Danbooru tags and tag categories.
  Source: Provided with the MIT-licensed source code of the upstream Langbai NovelAI Studio (https://github.com/2786886095/novelai-image-desktop).
- NovelAI quality tags, negative-prompt (UC) presets and model names in `electron/nai-payload.ts`, `src/prompt-normalize.ts` and `src/types.ts`.
  Source: The texts NovelAI's own image generator adds to its requests, reproduced in the code so that requests match it; they have not been checked line by line against NovelAI here.
- Optional Chinese tag dictionary DanbooruSearchOnline (`tags_enhanced.csv`).
  Source: **Not included in the app or its installers.** `novelai/scripts/install-tag-dictionary.mjs` downloads a pinned copy into the data folder only when the user runs it: https://github.com/SuzumiyaAkizuki/DanbooruSearchOnline (repository license GPL-3.0; third-party, partly machine-generated data).

Tag names, categories and post counts of Danbooru (https://danbooru.donmai.us) are facts looked up there; the app
queries Danbooru at run time for current values and does not ship Danbooru's database.
